Mode contract

recorded (outcome.json contract field)

Verbatim, as recorded with the run and sent to the agents.

- primitive to instantiate: `dsp58_int8_dot` (precision INT9)
- what it is: this mode copies the AMD DSP58 block in one configuration, "DSP_MODE = INT8, INT8 vector dot product" (AM004 v1.2.1 chapter 4 (p71-75), p9). It is not the whole DSP58: only the ports below exist, and the rest of the real block is fixed inside the mode (C = 0, ALUMODE = 0000, INMODE = 00000, CARRYINSEL = 000, CARRYIN = 0, D = 0, A[33:27] = 0, ASYNC_RST = 0, every cascade input 0, every clock enable and reset not listed as a port 0). Differences from the real block: the C port is tied to 0 inside the mode (the tile has 116 routable data inputs); OPMODE is restricted to the listed values; PCOUT, ACOUT, BCOUT, the carry, pattern-detect and XOR outputs are not exposed; timing figures and tile area are this project's, not AMD's.
- computes: P = a0*b0 + a1*b1 + a2*b2; the fields are a0 = A[8:0], a1 = A[17:9], a2 = A[26:18], b0 = B[7:0], b1 = B[15:8], b2 = B[23:16] (each a is a 9-bit and each b an 8-bit two's complement number). That is one of the forms the block takes: `OPMODE` chooses the form at every clock edge (listed below).
- input ports: CLK[1] (clock), A[27], B[24], OPMODE[9], NEGATE[3], RSTA[1], RSTB[1], RSTP[1], CEA2[1], CEB2[1], CEP[1]
- output ports: P[58]
- arithmetic: the block's operands and result are signed two's complement. A design operand narrower than its port must be widened to the port width: zero-extended if the design treats it as unsigned, sign-extended if signed. For unsigned data the top bit of each element's field must be zero (AM004 p103: "port A can hold up to three unsigned 8-bit values by setting the sign bits to 0 to indicate non-negative values."). So an unsigned operand may be at most 8 bits on each of a0, a1, a2 and 7 bits on each of b0, b1, b2; a wider unsigned operand has no exact representation on this block. P is a 58-bit value accumulated modulo 2^58. The result wraps at 58 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 58 bits (modulo 2^58), with no saturation and no flag.
- registers: on `CLK`, the block has exactly these registers. `A2` loads A at a clock edge where `CEA2` is high, and is cleared to 0 by `RSTA`. `B2` loads B at a clock edge where `CEB2` is high, and is cleared to 0 by `RSTB`. `P` loads the value OPMODE selects from the dot product of A2 and B2 (under NEGATE) and P at a clock edge where `CEP` is high, and is cleared to 0 by `RSTP`; the output `P` is this register. The block has no path around a register.
- timing: `A` and `B` reach `P` 2 clock edges after they are applied (applied before edge k, in `P` after edge k+1); `OPMODE` and `NEGATE` have no register before the one that drives `P`: the value present at a clock edge is in `P` after that edge (1 cycle).
- control `OPMODE`: a per-cycle input, not registered; it acts at the clock edge that loads P. Legal values, and what the clock edge then does:
  - 000000101 (dot product): P <= dot
  - 000100101 (accumulate): P <= P + dot
  - 010000000 (hold): P <= P
  - 000000000 (zero): P <= 0
  - any other value: a clock edge that loads P under it leaves P unknown (X); the mode does not define it.
- control `NEGATE`: a per-cycle input, not registered; it acts at the clock edge that loads P, on the products of the A2 and B2 contents at that edge. NEGATE[i] = 1 negates product i: dot = (NEGATE[0] ? -a0*b0 : a0*b0) + (NEGATE[1] ? -a1*b1 : a1*b1) + (NEGATE[2] ? -a2*b2 : a2*b2), with a and b the fields of A2 and B2. Every 3-bit value is legal.
- resets and clock enables (resets `RSTA`, `RSTB` and `RSTP`; clock enables `CEA2`, `CEB2` and `CEP`): each reset is synchronous and active high, clears only its own register to 0 at the clock edge that samples it high, and wins over that register's clock enable; each clock enable is active high; its register holds its value at a clock edge that samples the enable low. The block has no other reset and no other enable.
- power-up: every register of the block starts at 0 (as the vendor's simulation model starts them), so `P` is defined from the first clock edge with or without a reset.
- connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. A reset the rewrite does not need is tied to 0; a clock enable it does not need is tied to 1; `OPMODE` may be a constant legal value.

prompt
Task

You are the planner. Read the design source, raygentop.v, and decide which of the candidate arithmetic regions listed below to rewrite onto the hard block described next, or that none should be rewritten. Write a plan for the editor, who carries it out; do not edit any file.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: dsp58_int8_dot (precision INT9)
  • what it is: this mode copies the AMD DSP58 block in one configuration, "DSP_MODE = INT8, INT8 vector dot product" (AM004 v1.2.1 chapter 4 (p71-75), p9). It is not the whole DSP58: only the ports below exist, and the rest of the real block is fixed inside the mode (C = 0, ALUMODE = 0000, INMODE = 00000, CARRYINSEL = 000, CARRYIN = 0, D = 0, A[33:27] = 0, ASYNC_RST = 0, every cascade input 0, every clock enable and reset not listed as a port 0). Differences from the real block: the C port is tied to 0 inside the mode (the tile has 116 routable data inputs); OPMODE is restricted to the listed values; PCOUT, ACOUT, BCOUT, the carry, pattern-detect and XOR outputs are not exposed; timing figures and tile area are this project's, not AMD's.
  • computes: P = a0*b0 + a1*b1 + a2*b2; the fields are a0 = A[8:0], a1 = A[17:9], a2 = A[26:18], b0 = B[7:0], b1 = B[15:8], b2 = B[23:16] (each a is a 9-bit and each b an 8-bit two's complement number). That is one of the forms the block takes: OPMODE chooses the form at every clock edge (listed below).
  • input ports: CLK[1] (clock), A[27], B[24], OPMODE[9], NEGATE[3], RSTA[1], RSTB[1], RSTP[1], CEA2[1], CEB2[1], CEP[1]
  • output ports: P[58]
  • arithmetic: the block's operands and result are signed two's complement. A design operand narrower than its port must be widened to the port width: zero-extended if the design treats it as unsigned, sign-extended if signed. For unsigned data the top bit of each element's field must be zero (AM004 p103: "port A can hold up to three unsigned 8-bit values by setting the sign bits to 0 to indicate non-negative values."). So an unsigned operand may be at most 8 bits on each of a0, a1, a2 and 7 bits on each of b0, b1, b2; a wider unsigned operand has no exact representation on this block. P is a 58-bit value accumulated modulo 2^58. The result wraps at 58 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 58 bits (modulo 2^58), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on CLK, the block has exactly these registers. A2 loads A at a clock edge where CEA2 is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTA. B2 loads B at a clock edge where CEB2 is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTB. P loads the value OPMODE selects from the dot product of A2 and B2 (under NEGATE) and P at a clock edge where CEP is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTP; the output P is this register. The block has no path around a register.
  • timing: A and B reach P 2 clock edges after they are applied (applied before edge k, in P after edge k+1); OPMODE and NEGATE have no register before the one that drives P: the value present at a clock edge is in P after that edge (1 cycle).

  • control OPMODE: a per-cycle input, not registered; it acts at the clock edge that loads P. Legal values, and what the clock edge then does:

    • 000000101 (dot product): P <= dot
    • 000100101 (accumulate): P <= P + dot
    • 010000000 (hold): P <= P
    • 000000000 (zero): P <= 0
    • any other value: a clock edge that loads P under it leaves P unknown (X); the mode does not define it.
  • control NEGATE: a per-cycle input, not registered; it acts at the clock edge that loads P, on the products of the A2 and B2 contents at that edge. NEGATE[i] = 1 negates product i: dot = (NEGATE[0] ? -a0*b0 : a0*b0) + (NEGATE[1] ? -a1*b1 : a1*b1) + (NEGATE[2] ? -a2*b2 : a2*b2), with a and b the fields of A2 and B2. Every 3-bit value is legal.
  • resets and clock enables (resets RSTA, RSTB and RSTP; clock enables CEA2, CEB2 and CEP): each reset is synchronous and active high, clears only its own register to 0 at the clock edge that samples it high, and wins over that register's clock enable; each clock enable is active high; its register holds its value at a clock edge that samples the enable low. The block has no other reset and no other enable.
  • power-up: every register of the block starts at 0 (as the vendor's simulation model starts them), so P is defined from the first clock edge with or without a reset.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. A reset the rewrite does not need is tied to 0; a clock enable it does not need is tied to 1; OPMODE may be a constant legal value.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block at A, B, RSTA, RSTB, RSTP, CEA2, CEB2 and CEP: a path ends at a register of the block (setup)0.019
into the block at OPMODE and NEGATE, which have no input register: the path runs on through the block's delay to the register behind P (delay + setup)2.140 + 0.019 = 2.159
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of P (clock-to-Q)0.060
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path into OPMODE and NEGATE is not cut at the block's pins: it runs through the block to the register behind P, so the logic that drives them and the block's delay are in one stage.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design
Candidate regions

Multiplies in the unmodified raygentop, counted in its packed netlist at seed 1: 15 on 6 DSP tiles, of which 9 in mult_9x9_fixed_pt mode on 3 tiles (1 tile with 4, 1 with 3 and 1 with 2) and 6 in two_mult_18x19 mode on 3 tiles (2 to a tile). 18 products in the source become 15 multiply cells after synthesis. The DSP tile count is the same at each of the five seeds. Multiplies in soft logic, if any, are not in this count.

Each row is one multiply of the design as elaborated from the source. An id names it by its module and by the name the elaborated design gives the multiply, which carries the file and the line; multiplies that come from one line (several written on it, or one inside a generate loop) differ in the number after the last $. An operand's bits are the bits it carries, the zero or sign extension to the width of the expression left out; signed or unsigned is how the multiply reads it. Instances is how many instances of the multiply's module the design holds. Nothing else about a multiply is listed: what its product feeds is in the source.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's resets, clock enables or per-cycle controls, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's resets (RSTA, RSTB and RSTP), clock enables (CEA2, CEB2 and CEP) and per-cycle controls (OPMODE and NEGATE) so that "same behavior" holds after every reset of the design, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds in soft logic start unknown; do not rely on their initial value. The block's own registers start at 0, as its power-up line states.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Goal

Propose a correct rewrite onto the block. The loop measures it against the original design on area × delay; you are not asked to predict the result.

No rewrite
  • REGIONS: NONE: You propose no rewrite this time. The plan states why, from what you were given: no correct rewrite, or nothing left to try. The run then ends and keeps the best result so far if its median over every seed is below the original's, and the original design otherwise (also when no rewrite has beaten it).
What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left: a correctness reason, or "not chosen this pass"; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output rules
  • Write nothing before the REGIONS line; read the source first.
  • Your reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
  • If a change to the design itself, not to the rewrite, would let more of it use the block, you may say so on a line starting DESIGN_CHANGE:. It is reported to the user and does not change your decision.
Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output of bilinearintrp and matmult on every cycle after the testbench's lead-in phase (its first 8 cycles). A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_raygentop.v) and the block's behavioural model (dsp58_int8_dot.v) are in sim_reference/.

response

I'll start by reading the design source.

REGIONS: bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2875$349, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2876$350, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2879$353, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2882$356
SHARED_REDUCTION: the three output sums of bilinearintrp: r = i1r+i2r+i3r (raygentop.v:2884), g = i1g+i2g+i3g (2885), b = i1b+i2b+i3b (2886) — one block per sum
BLOCKS: 3
MAPPING:

  • Block dsp_r (red channel): CLK=clk; A = {1'b0, wnext, 1'b0, v, 1'b0, u} so a0=u(8b unsigned), a1=v(8b unsigned), a2=wnext(8b unsigned); B = {1'b0, rw, 1'b0, rv, 1'b0, ru} so b0=ru, b1=rv, b2=rw (7b unsigned each); OPMODE=9'b000000101; NEGATE=3'b000; RSTA=RSTB=RSTP=1'b0; CEA2=CEB2=CEP=1'b1; P → assign r = P[6:0].
  • Block dsp_g: same A, OPMODE, NEGATE, resets, enables; B = {1'b0, gw, 1'b0, gv, 1'b0, gu}; P → assign g = P[6:0].
  • Block dsp_b: same A, OPMODE, NEGATE, resets, enables; B = {1'b0, bw, 1'b0, bv, 1'b0, bu}; P → assign b = P[6:0].
  • wnext is new soft combinational logic: wire [7:0] wnext = 8'b11111111 - u - v; (the right-hand side of raygentop.v:2862, unregistered), with u, v the existing combinational mux outputs of the always @(selectuv or ...) block at raygentop.v:2818-2858, which stays exactly as it is.

PLAN:

What is converted and why it is exact

All nine multiplies in bilinearintrp (raygentop.v:2874-2882) are converted onto three dsp58_int8_dot blocks, one per colour channel. Each channel is already literally a 3-term dot product of three 8-bit unsigned values (ul, vl, wl) with three 7-bit unsigned values (rul/rvl/rwl, gul/…, bul/…), which is exactly the block's form: an unsigned operand may be 8 bits on each a field and 7 bits on each b field, so every operand fits with the required zero top bit and no decomposition or correction logic is needed.

Value equality: each product is at most 255*127 = 32385, exact in the 15-bit i* registers and exact in the block; the dot is at most 97155, which fits the model's 19-bit signed dot and the 58-bit P with no wrap. The outputs are 7-bit wires, so the original computes (i1r+i2r+i3r) in 15-bit arithmetic and keeps the low 7 bits; taking P[6:0] is the same value because reduction mod 2^15 then mod 2^7 equals reduction mod 2^7.

Visibility: ul, vl, wl, rul…bwl and i1r…i3b are declared at raygentop.v:2794-2816 and are read nowhere except inside the nine multiplies and the three output sums (raygentop.v:2874-2886); they are not ports and leave the module nowhere else. u and v stay as soft combinational signals (they also feed wnext), so nothing readable disappears into a block.

Edits for the editor (module bilinearintrp only, raygentop.v:2762-2887; the port list and port declarations are untouched):

  1. Delete the register declarations ul, vl, wl, i1b, i2b, i3b, i1g, i2g, i3g, i1r, i2r, i3r, rul, rvl, rwl, gul, gvl, gwl, bul, bvl, bwl (raygentop.v:2796-2816). Keep reg [7:0] u; reg [7:0] v;.
  2. Keep the combinational always @(selectuv or …) block (2818-2858) unchanged.
  3. Delete the whole always @(posedge clk) block (2860-2883) — both the latch-in assignments and the nine multiplies — and the three assign statements at 2884-2886.
  4. Add wire [7:0] wnext = 8'b11111111 - u - v; and wire [26:0] dot_a = {1'b0, wnext, 1'b0, v, 1'b0, u};.
  5. Instantiate the three blocks as in MAPPING, with wire [57:0] pr, pg, pb;, then assign r = pr[6:0]; assign g = pg[6:0]; assign b = pb[6:0];.
  6. Every input port of every block is connected by name as listed: unused resets tied to 1'b0, unused enables tied to 1'b1, OPMODE the constant legal value 9'b000000101 (dot product), NEGATE 3'b000 (all three products added).

Registers, reset and start-up

bilinearintrp has no reset port and no clock enable: at every clock edge the original loads ul/vl/wl/rul…bwl from u/v/255-u-v/ru… and loads i1r…i3b from the products of the previous edge's values. The block's A2/B2 replace the first rank (hence CEA2=CEB2=1, RSTA=RSTB=0) and P replaces the sum of the second rank (CEP=1, RSTP=0). wl is replaced by wnext registered inside A2, which is the same register stage as the original wl. All three blocks register their own copy of the same dot_a, matching the single shared ul/vl/wl rank. The design's registers start at 0 and the block's A2, B2, P start at 0, so r, g, b are 0 on the same cycles at start-up; since no reset of the design exists here, there is nothing further to reproduce, and the match holds on every cycle including the testbench's lead-in and drain phases.

Register stages, operands to result

  • Before: u/v/ru… → stage 1 (ul/vl/wl/rul… at raygentop.v:2862-2873) → stage 2 (i1r…i3b at 2874-2882) → combinational 3-input 15-bit add → r/g/b. Two register stages plus an adder on the output path.
  • After: u/v/ru… → stage 1 (A2/B2) → stage 2 (P, which holds the dot product itself) → bit slice P[6:0] → r/g/b. Two register stages, same latency on every cycle; the output adder is gone (the sum happens inside the block), so the path leaving the module is just P clock-to-Q.

Candidates left, with reasons

The nine matmult multiplies (raygentop.v:335-343, ids matmult.$mul$raygentop.v:335$15 through …343$23) are left, all for one reason: both operands are 16-bit unsigned, and this block represents an unsigned operand exactly only up to 8 bits on a0/a1/a2 and 7 bits on b0/b1/b2, so no 16x16 product fits on a block. An exact rewrite would need each operand split (2 chunks of 8 bits on A, 3 chunks of at most 7 bits on B) = 6 partial products per multiply, 54 in total = 18 blocks plus soft shift-and-add trees, against the 3 DSP tiles these multiplies occupy now. Furthermore the design truncates each product on its own before summing — Cx <= am11[30:15] + am12[30:15] + am13[30:15] (raygentop.v:348-350), so the low 15 bits are discarded per product — which means the block's accumulate mode cannot stand in for the channel sum anyway and each product would have to come out of the blocks separately. Not chosen: it is a large area loss with no path to a gain.

DESIGN_CHANGE: in matmult, the three channel sums discard the low 15 bits of each product before adding (raygentop.v:348-350). If the design instead summed the full 32-bit products and truncated once (Cx <= (am11+am12+am13)[30:15]), each channel would be a single dot product and the per-product partial sums could be accumulated inside one block per channel; the 16-bit operand widths would still need splitting, so the bigger win would come from narrowing Ax/Ay/Az to 8 bits unsigned and m11…m33 to 7 bits unsigned, after which each of Cx, Cy, Cz is exactly one dsp58_int8_dot, taking matmult from 3 DSP tiles to 3 with the adders removed, or from 9 multiplies to 3 dot products. Both are changes to what the design computes and are not part of this rewrite.

1	// dsp58_int8_dot: behavioural model of the arch mode dsp58_int8_dot_mode, which mimics the AMD DSP58
2	// with DSP_MODE = INT8, the INT8 vector dot product (AM004 v1.2.1 chapter 4), attributes AREG = BREG = 1,
3	// ACASCREG = BCASCREG = 1, MREG = 0, PREG = 1, OPMODEREG = 0, INMODEREG = 0, USE_MULT = MULTIPLY,
4	// AMULTSEL = A, BMULTSEL = B, ALUMODE = 0000, INMODE = 00000, CARRYINSEL = 000, CARRYIN = 0, and the
5	// C port tied to 0 inside the mode. Reference for every per-cycle question: AMD's simulation model
6	// /tools/Xilinx/Vivado/2024.1/data/verilog/src/unisims/DSP58.v (Vivado 2024.1; registers start at 0 in the
7	// initial block at lines 1241-1271), read in place and never copied.
8	// Element i (i = 0, 1, 2): a_i = A[9i+8:9i], 9 bits signed; b_i = B[8i+7:8i], 8 bits signed.
9	// dot = +/-a0*b0 +/- a1*b1 +/- a2*b2, where NEGATE[i] = 1 negates product i.
10	// Per clock edge: A2 <= A if CEA2 (cleared by RSTA); B2 <= B if CEB2 (cleared by RSTB);
11	// P <= f(OPMODE, dot of A2, B2 under NEGATE, P) if CEP (cleared by RSTP). A, B to P: 2 cycles; OPMODE
12	// and NEGATE to P: 1 cycle (they are not registered). P wraps at 58 bits. OPMODE is restricted to the
13	// four values below; on any other value at an edge that loads P, P becomes X.
14	// Signed two's complement throughout, as the real block; unsigned data must have a zero top bit on
15	// each operand. Every register starts at 0: AMD's simulation model starts its registers at 0 in the
16	// `initial` block(s) cited below (Vivado 2024.1). Resets are synchronous, active high, clear only the
17	// register(s) they name and win over that register's clock enable. Timing figures and tile area are
18	// this project's arch's own, not AMD's. One block per DSP tile.
19	module dsp58_int8_dot (
20	    input CLK,
21	    input [26:0] A,
22	    input [23:0] B,
23	    input [8:0] OPMODE,
24	    input [2:0] NEGATE,
25	    input RSTA, RSTB, RSTP, CEA2, CEB2, CEP,
26	    output [57:0] P
27	);
28	    reg [26:0] a2 = 0;
29	    reg [23:0] b2 = 0;
30	    reg [57:0] p = 0;
31	    wire signed [18:0] m0 = $signed(a2[8:0])   * $signed(b2[7:0]);     // 9x8 signed, exact
32	    wire signed [18:0] m1 = $signed(a2[17:9])  * $signed(b2[15:8]);
33	    wire signed [18:0] m2 = $signed(a2[26:18]) * $signed(b2[23:16]);
34	    wire signed [18:0] dot = (NEGATE[0] ? -m0 : m0) + (NEGATE[1] ? -m1 : m1) + (NEGATE[2] ? -m2 : m2);
35	    wire [57:0] mx = {{39{dot[18]}}, dot};
36	    // what P loads, as a function so that it is defined from time 0
37	    function [57:0] alu;
38	        input [8:0] opmode;
39	        input [57:0] p;
40	        input [57:0] mx;
41	        case (opmode)
42	            9'b000000101: alu = mx;            // dot product
43	            9'b000100101: alu = p + mx;        // accumulate (Z = P)
44	            9'b010000000: alu = p;             // hold through the ALU (W = P)
45	            9'b000000000: alu = 58'd0;         // zero
46	            default:      alu = {58{1'bx}};    // not a value of this mode
47	        endcase
48	    endfunction
49	    wire [57:0] nxt = alu(OPMODE, p, mx);
50	    always @(posedge CLK) begin
51	        if (RSTA) a2 <= 0; else if (CEA2) a2 <= A;
52	        if (RSTB) b2 <= 0; else if (CEB2) b2 <= B;
53	        if (RSTP) p <= 0; else if (CEP) p <= nxt;
54	    end
55	    assign P = p;
56	endmodule

4	// Compares every output every cycle: r[7], g[7], b[7], matmult__Cx[16], matmult__Cy[16], matmult__Cz[16].

Task

You are the plan reviewer. You did not write this plan. Check it against the design source, the block's contract and the correctness rules; approve it if it violates none.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: dsp58_int8_dot (precision INT9)
  • what it is: this mode copies the AMD DSP58 block in one configuration, "DSP_MODE = INT8, INT8 vector dot product" (AM004 v1.2.1 chapter 4 (p71-75), p9). It is not the whole DSP58: only the ports below exist, and the rest of the real block is fixed inside the mode (C = 0, ALUMODE = 0000, INMODE = 00000, CARRYINSEL = 000, CARRYIN = 0, D = 0, A[33:27] = 0, ASYNC_RST = 0, every cascade input 0, every clock enable and reset not listed as a port 0). Differences from the real block: the C port is tied to 0 inside the mode (the tile has 116 routable data inputs); OPMODE is restricted to the listed values; PCOUT, ACOUT, BCOUT, the carry, pattern-detect and XOR outputs are not exposed; timing figures and tile area are this project's, not AMD's.
  • computes: P = a0*b0 + a1*b1 + a2*b2; the fields are a0 = A[8:0], a1 = A[17:9], a2 = A[26:18], b0 = B[7:0], b1 = B[15:8], b2 = B[23:16] (each a is a 9-bit and each b an 8-bit two's complement number). That is one of the forms the block takes: OPMODE chooses the form at every clock edge (listed below).
  • input ports: CLK[1] (clock), A[27], B[24], OPMODE[9], NEGATE[3], RSTA[1], RSTB[1], RSTP[1], CEA2[1], CEB2[1], CEP[1]
  • output ports: P[58]
  • arithmetic: the block's operands and result are signed two's complement. A design operand narrower than its port must be widened to the port width: zero-extended if the design treats it as unsigned, sign-extended if signed. For unsigned data the top bit of each element's field must be zero (AM004 p103: "port A can hold up to three unsigned 8-bit values by setting the sign bits to 0 to indicate non-negative values."). So an unsigned operand may be at most 8 bits on each of a0, a1, a2 and 7 bits on each of b0, b1, b2; a wider unsigned operand has no exact representation on this block. P is a 58-bit value accumulated modulo 2^58. The result wraps at 58 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 58 bits (modulo 2^58), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on CLK, the block has exactly these registers. A2 loads A at a clock edge where CEA2 is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTA. B2 loads B at a clock edge where CEB2 is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTB. P loads the value OPMODE selects from the dot product of A2 and B2 (under NEGATE) and P at a clock edge where CEP is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTP; the output P is this register. The block has no path around a register.
  • timing: A and B reach P 2 clock edges after they are applied (applied before edge k, in P after edge k+1); OPMODE and NEGATE have no register before the one that drives P: the value present at a clock edge is in P after that edge (1 cycle).

  • control OPMODE: a per-cycle input, not registered; it acts at the clock edge that loads P. Legal values, and what the clock edge then does:

    • 000000101 (dot product): P <= dot
    • 000100101 (accumulate): P <= P + dot
    • 010000000 (hold): P <= P
    • 000000000 (zero): P <= 0
    • any other value: a clock edge that loads P under it leaves P unknown (X); the mode does not define it.
  • control NEGATE: a per-cycle input, not registered; it acts at the clock edge that loads P, on the products of the A2 and B2 contents at that edge. NEGATE[i] = 1 negates product i: dot = (NEGATE[0] ? -a0*b0 : a0*b0) + (NEGATE[1] ? -a1*b1 : a1*b1) + (NEGATE[2] ? -a2*b2 : a2*b2), with a and b the fields of A2 and B2. Every 3-bit value is legal.
  • resets and clock enables (resets RSTA, RSTB and RSTP; clock enables CEA2, CEB2 and CEP): each reset is synchronous and active high, clears only its own register to 0 at the clock edge that samples it high, and wins over that register's clock enable; each clock enable is active high; its register holds its value at a clock edge that samples the enable low. The block has no other reset and no other enable.
  • power-up: every register of the block starts at 0 (as the vendor's simulation model starts them), so P is defined from the first clock edge with or without a reset.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. A reset the rewrite does not need is tied to 0; a clock enable it does not need is tied to 1; OPMODE may be a constant legal value.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block at A, B, RSTA, RSTB, RSTP, CEA2, CEB2 and CEP: a path ends at a register of the block (setup)0.019
into the block at OPMODE and NEGATE, which have no input register: the path runs on through the block's delay to the register behind P (delay + setup)2.140 + 0.019 = 2.159
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of P (clock-to-Q)0.060
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path into OPMODE and NEGATE is not cut at the block's pins: it runs through the block to the register behind P, so the logic that drives them and the block's delay are in one stage.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design

The design source is raygentop.v. The candidate regions the planner was offered:

Multiplies in the unmodified raygentop, counted in its packed netlist at seed 1: 15 on 6 DSP tiles, of which 9 in mult_9x9_fixed_pt mode on 3 tiles (1 tile with 4, 1 with 3 and 1 with 2) and 6 in two_mult_18x19 mode on 3 tiles (2 to a tile). 18 products in the source become 15 multiply cells after synthesis. The DSP tile count is the same at each of the five seeds. Multiplies in soft logic, if any, are not in this count.

Each row is one multiply of the design as elaborated from the source. An id names it by its module and by the name the elaborated design gives the multiply, which carries the file and the line; multiplies that come from one line (several written on it, or one inside a generate loop) differ in the number after the last $. An operand's bits are the bits it carries, the zero or sign extension to the width of the expression left out; signed or unsigned is how the multiply reads it. Instances is how many instances of the multiply's module the design holds. Nothing else about a multiply is listed: what its product feeds is in the source.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's resets, clock enables or per-cycle controls, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's resets (RSTA, RSTB and RSTP), clock enables (CEA2, CEB2 and CEP) and per-cycle controls (OPMODE and NEGATE) so that "same behavior" holds after every reset of the design, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds in soft logic start unknown; do not rely on their initial value. The block's own registers start at 0, as its power-up line states.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Check
  • Every claim the plan makes about the design (who reads a value, a register's reset or enable, widths, cited lines, ASSUMES lines) against the source.
  • That the mapping as described keeps the same behavior.
  • That the plan states everything the planner's output section requires, listed below.

Do not judge cost or style; measurement decides cost.

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left: a correctness reason, or "not chosen this pass"; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output
  • First line exactly APPROVE or REVISE.
  • If REVISE, for each problem: the rule it violates or the required item it omits, by name; the design lines; what to change.
  • Write nothing before the verdict line; read the source first. The verdict is applied automatically, and your reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The plan

I'll start by reading the design source.

REGIONS: bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2875$349, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2876$350, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2879$353, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2882$356
SHARED_REDUCTION: the three output sums of bilinearintrp: r = i1r+i2r+i3r (raygentop.v:2884), g = i1g+i2g+i3g (2885), b = i1b+i2b+i3b (2886) — one block per sum
BLOCKS: 3
MAPPING:

  • Block dsp_r (red channel): CLK=clk; A = {1'b0, wnext, 1'b0, v, 1'b0, u} so a0=u(8b unsigned), a1=v(8b unsigned), a2=wnext(8b unsigned); B = {1'b0, rw, 1'b0, rv, 1'b0, ru} so b0=ru, b1=rv, b2=rw (7b unsigned each); OPMODE=9'b000000101; NEGATE=3'b000; RSTA=RSTB=RSTP=1'b0; CEA2=CEB2=CEP=1'b1; P → assign r = P[6:0].
  • Block dsp_g: same A, OPMODE, NEGATE, resets, enables; B = {1'b0, gw, 1'b0, gv, 1'b0, gu}; P → assign g = P[6:0].
  • Block dsp_b: same A, OPMODE, NEGATE, resets, enables; B = {1'b0, bw, 1'b0, bv, 1'b0, bu}; P → assign b = P[6:0].
  • wnext is new soft combinational logic: wire [7:0] wnext = 8'b11111111 - u - v; (the right-hand side of raygentop.v:2862, unregistered), with u, v the existing combinational mux outputs of the always @(selectuv or ...) block at raygentop.v:2818-2858, which stays exactly as it is.

PLAN:

What is converted and why it is exact

All nine multiplies in bilinearintrp (raygentop.v:2874-2882) are converted onto three dsp58_int8_dot blocks, one per colour channel. Each channel is already literally a 3-term dot product of three 8-bit unsigned values (ul, vl, wl) with three 7-bit unsigned values (rul/rvl/rwl, gul/…, bul/…), which is exactly the block's form: an unsigned operand may be 8 bits on each a field and 7 bits on each b field, so every operand fits with the required zero top bit and no decomposition or correction logic is needed.

Value equality: each product is at most 255*127 = 32385, exact in the 15-bit i* registers and exact in the block; the dot is at most 97155, which fits the model's 19-bit signed dot and the 58-bit P with no wrap. The outputs are 7-bit wires, so the original computes (i1r+i2r+i3r) in 15-bit arithmetic and keeps the low 7 bits; taking P[6:0] is the same value because reduction mod 2^15 then mod 2^7 equals reduction mod 2^7.

Visibility: ul, vl, wl, rul…bwl and i1r…i3b are declared at raygentop.v:2794-2816 and are read nowhere except inside the nine multiplies and the three output sums (raygentop.v:2874-2886); they are not ports and leave the module nowhere else. u and v stay as soft combinational signals (they also feed wnext), so nothing readable disappears into a block.

Edits for the editor (module bilinearintrp only, raygentop.v:2762-2887; the port list and port declarations are untouched):

  1. Delete the register declarations ul, vl, wl, i1b, i2b, i3b, i1g, i2g, i3g, i1r, i2r, i3r, rul, rvl, rwl, gul, gvl, gwl, bul, bvl, bwl (raygentop.v:2796-2816). Keep reg [7:0] u; reg [7:0] v;.
  2. Keep the combinational always @(selectuv or …) block (2818-2858) unchanged.
  3. Delete the whole always @(posedge clk) block (2860-2883) — both the latch-in assignments and the nine multiplies — and the three assign statements at 2884-2886.
  4. Add wire [7:0] wnext = 8'b11111111 - u - v; and wire [26:0] dot_a = {1'b0, wnext, 1'b0, v, 1'b0, u};.
  5. Instantiate the three blocks as in MAPPING, with wire [57:0] pr, pg, pb;, then assign r = pr[6:0]; assign g = pg[6:0]; assign b = pb[6:0];.
  6. Every input port of every block is connected by name as listed: unused resets tied to 1'b0, unused enables tied to 1'b1, OPMODE the constant legal value 9'b000000101 (dot product), NEGATE 3'b000 (all three products added).

Registers, reset and start-up

bilinearintrp has no reset port and no clock enable: at every clock edge the original loads ul/vl/wl/rul…bwl from u/v/255-u-v/ru… and loads i1r…i3b from the products of the previous edge's values. The block's A2/B2 replace the first rank (hence CEA2=CEB2=1, RSTA=RSTB=0) and P replaces the sum of the second rank (CEP=1, RSTP=0). wl is replaced by wnext registered inside A2, which is the same register stage as the original wl. All three blocks register their own copy of the same dot_a, matching the single shared ul/vl/wl rank. The design's registers start at 0 and the block's A2, B2, P start at 0, so r, g, b are 0 on the same cycles at start-up; since no reset of the design exists here, there is nothing further to reproduce, and the match holds on every cycle including the testbench's lead-in and drain phases.

Register stages, operands to result

  • Before: u/v/ru… → stage 1 (ul/vl/wl/rul… at raygentop.v:2862-2873) → stage 2 (i1r…i3b at 2874-2882) → combinational 3-input 15-bit add → r/g/b. Two register stages plus an adder on the output path.
  • After: u/v/ru… → stage 1 (A2/B2) → stage 2 (P, which holds the dot product itself) → bit slice P[6:0] → r/g/b. Two register stages, same latency on every cycle; the output adder is gone (the sum happens inside the block), so the path leaving the module is just P clock-to-Q.

Candidates left, with reasons

The nine matmult multiplies (raygentop.v:335-343, ids matmult.$mul$raygentop.v:335$15 through …343$23) are left, all for one reason: both operands are 16-bit unsigned, and this block represents an unsigned operand exactly only up to 8 bits on a0/a1/a2 and 7 bits on b0/b1/b2, so no 16x16 product fits on a block. An exact rewrite would need each operand split (2 chunks of 8 bits on A, 3 chunks of at most 7 bits on B) = 6 partial products per multiply, 54 in total = 18 blocks plus soft shift-and-add trees, against the 3 DSP tiles these multiplies occupy now. Furthermore the design truncates each product on its own before summing — Cx <= am11[30:15] + am12[30:15] + am13[30:15] (raygentop.v:348-350), so the low 15 bits are discarded per product — which means the block's accumulate mode cannot stand in for the channel sum anyway and each product would have to come out of the blocks separately. Not chosen: it is a large area loss with no path to a gain.

DESIGN_CHANGE: in matmult, the three channel sums discard the low 15 bits of each product before adding (raygentop.v:348-350). If the design instead summed the full 32-bit products and truncated once (Cx <= (am11+am12+am13)[30:15]), each channel would be a single dot product and the per-product partial sums could be accumulated inside one block per channel; the 16-bit operand widths would still need splitting, so the bigger win would come from narrowing Ax/Ay/Az to 8 bits unsigned and m11…m33 to 7 bits unsigned, after which each of Cx, Cy, Cz is exactly one dsp58_int8_dot, taking matmult from 3 DSP tiles to 3 with the adders removed, or from 9 multiplies to 3 dot products. Both are changes to what the design computes and are not part of this rewrite.

Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output of bilinearintrp and matmult on every cycle after the testbench's lead-in phase (its first 8 cycles). A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_raygentop.v) and the block's behavioural model (dsp58_int8_dot.v) are in sim_reference/.

Read these files in this directory: raygentop.v

APPROVE

Verification notes (source-checked, not objections):

  • Visibility / readers — ul, vl, wl, rul…bwl, i1r…i3b are declared at raygentop.v:2796-2816 and read only at raygentop.v:2874-2886. The ul/vl hits at raygentop.v:2706-2756 are a different module's 7-bit regs and are untouched by edits confined to bilinearintrp (2762-2887). u/v are read only at 2862-2864 and stay in soft logic. r/g/b (2786-2791) are the module's only outputs; one instance at raygentop.v:2012.
  • Field packing — {1'b0,wnext,1'b0,v,1'b0,u} gives a0=A[8:0]={0,u}, a1=A[17:9]={0,v}, a2=A[26:18]={0,wnext}; {1'b0,rw,1'b0,rv,1'b0,ru} gives b0={0,ru}, b1={0,rv}, b2={0,rw}. Pairings match 2874/2875/2876 (and the g/b rows). Unsigned widths are 8 on each a and 7 on each b, inside the contract's exact-representation limit with the required zero top bit.
  • Value equality — each product ≤ 255·127 = 32385, exact in the 15-bit i* regs; dot ≤ 97155, no wrap in 58 bits. Original assign r = (i1r+i2r+i3r) evaluates at 15 bits then keeps 7, and (S mod 2^15) mod 2^7 = S mod 2^7, so P[6:0] is identical. wire [7:0] wnext = 8'b11111111 - u - v; reproduces line 2862's 8-bit truncation.
  • Registers/reset — bilinearintrp has no reset and no enable (only always @(posedge clk) at 2860-2883), so RSTA/RSTB/RSTP=0, CEA2/CEB2/CEP=1 is right; stage count 2 before (2862-2873 then 2874-2882) and 2 after (A2/B2 then P), with the output adder absorbed. Block registers and design registers both start at 0, so cycle-0 outputs agree. No ASSUMES: is needed, correctly none is given.

Task

Edit raygentop.v in this directory to carry out the approved plan below: rewrite the regions it names onto the hard block described next. If a step cannot be done as the plan says, stop and say which step and why; do not improvise a different rewrite.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: dsp58_int8_dot (precision INT9)
  • what it is: this mode copies the AMD DSP58 block in one configuration, "DSP_MODE = INT8, INT8 vector dot product" (AM004 v1.2.1 chapter 4 (p71-75), p9). It is not the whole DSP58: only the ports below exist, and the rest of the real block is fixed inside the mode (C = 0, ALUMODE = 0000, INMODE = 00000, CARRYINSEL = 000, CARRYIN = 0, D = 0, A[33:27] = 0, ASYNC_RST = 0, every cascade input 0, every clock enable and reset not listed as a port 0). Differences from the real block: the C port is tied to 0 inside the mode (the tile has 116 routable data inputs); OPMODE is restricted to the listed values; PCOUT, ACOUT, BCOUT, the carry, pattern-detect and XOR outputs are not exposed; timing figures and tile area are this project's, not AMD's.
  • computes: P = a0*b0 + a1*b1 + a2*b2; the fields are a0 = A[8:0], a1 = A[17:9], a2 = A[26:18], b0 = B[7:0], b1 = B[15:8], b2 = B[23:16] (each a is a 9-bit and each b an 8-bit two's complement number). That is one of the forms the block takes: OPMODE chooses the form at every clock edge (listed below).
  • input ports: CLK[1] (clock), A[27], B[24], OPMODE[9], NEGATE[3], RSTA[1], RSTB[1], RSTP[1], CEA2[1], CEB2[1], CEP[1]
  • output ports: P[58]
  • arithmetic: the block's operands and result are signed two's complement. A design operand narrower than its port must be widened to the port width: zero-extended if the design treats it as unsigned, sign-extended if signed. For unsigned data the top bit of each element's field must be zero (AM004 p103: "port A can hold up to three unsigned 8-bit values by setting the sign bits to 0 to indicate non-negative values."). So an unsigned operand may be at most 8 bits on each of a0, a1, a2 and 7 bits on each of b0, b1, b2; a wider unsigned operand has no exact representation on this block. P is a 58-bit value accumulated modulo 2^58. The result wraps at 58 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 58 bits (modulo 2^58), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on CLK, the block has exactly these registers. A2 loads A at a clock edge where CEA2 is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTA. B2 loads B at a clock edge where CEB2 is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTB. P loads the value OPMODE selects from the dot product of A2 and B2 (under NEGATE) and P at a clock edge where CEP is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTP; the output P is this register. The block has no path around a register.
  • timing: A and B reach P 2 clock edges after they are applied (applied before edge k, in P after edge k+1); OPMODE and NEGATE have no register before the one that drives P: the value present at a clock edge is in P after that edge (1 cycle).

  • control OPMODE: a per-cycle input, not registered; it acts at the clock edge that loads P. Legal values, and what the clock edge then does:

    • 000000101 (dot product): P <= dot
    • 000100101 (accumulate): P <= P + dot
    • 010000000 (hold): P <= P
    • 000000000 (zero): P <= 0
    • any other value: a clock edge that loads P under it leaves P unknown (X); the mode does not define it.
  • control NEGATE: a per-cycle input, not registered; it acts at the clock edge that loads P, on the products of the A2 and B2 contents at that edge. NEGATE[i] = 1 negates product i: dot = (NEGATE[0] ? -a0*b0 : a0*b0) + (NEGATE[1] ? -a1*b1 : a1*b1) + (NEGATE[2] ? -a2*b2 : a2*b2), with a and b the fields of A2 and B2. Every 3-bit value is legal.
  • resets and clock enables (resets RSTA, RSTB and RSTP; clock enables CEA2, CEB2 and CEP): each reset is synchronous and active high, clears only its own register to 0 at the clock edge that samples it high, and wins over that register's clock enable; each clock enable is active high; its register holds its value at a clock edge that samples the enable low. The block has no other reset and no other enable.
  • power-up: every register of the block starts at 0 (as the vendor's simulation model starts them), so P is defined from the first clock edge with or without a reset.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. A reset the rewrite does not need is tied to 0; a clock enable it does not need is tied to 1; OPMODE may be a constant legal value.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block at A, B, RSTA, RSTB, RSTP, CEA2, CEB2 and CEP: a path ends at a register of the block (setup)0.019
into the block at OPMODE and NEGATE, which have no input register: the path runs on through the block's delay to the register behind P (delay + setup)2.140 + 0.019 = 2.159
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of P (clock-to-Q)0.060
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path into OPMODE and NEGATE is not cut at the block's pins: it runs through the block to the register behind P, so the logic that drives them and the block's delay are in one stage.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design
  • Multiplies in the approved plan: bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2875$349, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2876$350, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2879$353, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2882$356. The plan names their shared sum as 'the three output sums of bilinearintrp: r = i1r+i2r+i3r (raygentop.v:2884), g = i1g+i2g+i3g (2885), b = i1b+i2b+i3b (2886) — one block per sum'.
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's resets, clock enables or per-cycle controls, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's resets (RSTA, RSTB and RSTP), clock enables (CEA2, CEB2 and CEP) and per-cycle controls (OPMODE and NEGATE) so that "same behavior" holds after every reset of the design, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds in soft logic start unknown; do not rely on their initial value. The block's own registers start at 0, as its power-up line states.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
How to work
  • After each edit, run bash check.sh raygentop.v and read its output. If it fails, fix the RTL and run it again.
  • check.sh reads the architecture's declaration of every hard block. Instantiate the block by its name and do not declare it in the design: a design that defines a module of the block's name fails the check.
  • Do not run place-and-route or any VTR command; that is done after you finish.
  • Stop when check.sh passes and its cell census shows the block instances the plan calls for.
Output
  • State what you changed, region by region, and stop.
  • Your edit is checked automatically, and this reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The approved plan

I'll start by reading the design source.

REGIONS: bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2875$349, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2876$350, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2879$353, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2882$356

BLOCKS: 3
MAPPING:

  • Block dsp_r (red channel): CLK=clk; A = {1'b0, wnext, 1'b0, v, 1'b0, u} so a0=u(8b unsigned), a1=v(8b unsigned), a2=wnext(8b unsigned); B = {1'b0, rw, 1'b0, rv, 1'b0, ru} so b0=ru, b1=rv, b2=rw (7b unsigned each); OPMODE=9'b000000101; NEGATE=3'b000; RSTA=RSTB=RSTP=1'b0; CEA2=CEB2=CEP=1'b1; P → assign r = P[6:0].
  • Block dsp_g: same A, OPMODE, NEGATE, resets, enables; B = {1'b0, gw, 1'b0, gv, 1'b0, gu}; P → assign g = P[6:0].
  • Block dsp_b: same A, OPMODE, NEGATE, resets, enables; B = {1'b0, bw, 1'b0, bv, 1'b0, bu}; P → assign b = P[6:0].
  • wnext is new soft combinational logic: wire [7:0] wnext = 8'b11111111 - u - v; (the right-hand side of raygentop.v:2862, unregistered), with u, v the existing combinational mux outputs of the always @(selectuv or ...) block at raygentop.v:2818-2858, which stays exactly as it is.

PLAN:

What is converted and why it is exact

All nine multiplies in bilinearintrp (raygentop.v:2874-2882) are converted onto three dsp58_int8_dot blocks, one per colour channel. Each channel is already literally a 3-term dot product of three 8-bit unsigned values (ul, vl, wl) with three 7-bit unsigned values (rul/rvl/rwl, gul/…, bul/…), which is exactly the block's form: an unsigned operand may be 8 bits on each a field and 7 bits on each b field, so every operand fits with the required zero top bit and no decomposition or correction logic is needed.

Value equality: each product is at most 255*127 = 32385, exact in the 15-bit i* registers and exact in the block; the dot is at most 97155, which fits the model's 19-bit signed dot and the 58-bit P with no wrap. The outputs are 7-bit wires, so the original computes (i1r+i2r+i3r) in 15-bit arithmetic and keeps the low 7 bits; taking P[6:0] is the same value because reduction mod 2^15 then mod 2^7 equals reduction mod 2^7.

Visibility: ul, vl, wl, rul…bwl and i1r…i3b are declared at raygentop.v:2794-2816 and are read nowhere except inside the nine multiplies and the three output sums (raygentop.v:2874-2886); they are not ports and leave the module nowhere else. u and v stay as soft combinational signals (they also feed wnext), so nothing readable disappears into a block.

Edits for the editor (module bilinearintrp only, raygentop.v:2762-2887; the port list and port declarations are untouched):

  1. Delete the register declarations ul, vl, wl, i1b, i2b, i3b, i1g, i2g, i3g, i1r, i2r, i3r, rul, rvl, rwl, gul, gvl, gwl, bul, bvl, bwl (raygentop.v:2796-2816). Keep reg [7:0] u; reg [7:0] v;.
  2. Keep the combinational always @(selectuv or …) block (2818-2858) unchanged.
  3. Delete the whole always @(posedge clk) block (2860-2883) — both the latch-in assignments and the nine multiplies — and the three assign statements at 2884-2886.
  4. Add wire [7:0] wnext = 8'b11111111 - u - v; and wire [26:0] dot_a = {1'b0, wnext, 1'b0, v, 1'b0, u};.
  5. Instantiate the three blocks as in MAPPING, with wire [57:0] pr, pg, pb;, then assign r = pr[6:0]; assign g = pg[6:0]; assign b = pb[6:0];.
  6. Every input port of every block is connected by name as listed: unused resets tied to 1'b0, unused enables tied to 1'b1, OPMODE the constant legal value 9'b000000101 (dot product), NEGATE 3'b000 (all three products added).

Registers, reset and start-up

bilinearintrp has no reset port and no clock enable: at every clock edge the original loads ul/vl/wl/rul…bwl from u/v/255-u-v/ru… and loads i1r…i3b from the products of the previous edge's values. The block's A2/B2 replace the first rank (hence CEA2=CEB2=1, RSTA=RSTB=0) and P replaces the sum of the second rank (CEP=1, RSTP=0). wl is replaced by wnext registered inside A2, which is the same register stage as the original wl. All three blocks register their own copy of the same dot_a, matching the single shared ul/vl/wl rank. The design's registers start at 0 and the block's A2, B2, P start at 0, so r, g, b are 0 on the same cycles at start-up; since no reset of the design exists here, there is nothing further to reproduce, and the match holds on every cycle including the testbench's lead-in and drain phases.

Register stages, operands to result

  • Before: u/v/ru… → stage 1 (ul/vl/wl/rul… at raygentop.v:2862-2873) → stage 2 (i1r…i3b at 2874-2882) → combinational 3-input 15-bit add → r/g/b. Two register stages plus an adder on the output path.
  • After: u/v/ru… → stage 1 (A2/B2) → stage 2 (P, which holds the dot product itself) → bit slice P[6:0] → r/g/b. Two register stages, same latency on every cycle; the output adder is gone (the sum happens inside the block), so the path leaving the module is just P clock-to-Q.

Candidates left, with reasons

The nine matmult multiplies (raygentop.v:335-343, ids matmult.$mul$raygentop.v:335$15 through …343$23) are left, all for one reason: both operands are 16-bit unsigned, and this block represents an unsigned operand exactly only up to 8 bits on a0/a1/a2 and 7 bits on b0/b1/b2, so no 16x16 product fits on a block. An exact rewrite would need each operand split (2 chunks of 8 bits on A, 3 chunks of at most 7 bits on B) = 6 partial products per multiply, 54 in total = 18 blocks plus soft shift-and-add trees, against the 3 DSP tiles these multiplies occupy now. Furthermore the design truncates each product on its own before summing — Cx <= am11[30:15] + am12[30:15] + am13[30:15] (raygentop.v:348-350), so the low 15 bits are discarded per product — which means the block's accumulate mode cannot stand in for the channel sum anyway and each product would have to come out of the blocks separately. Not chosen: it is a large area loss with no path to a gain.

Simulation

When you finish, the framework simulates your edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output of bilinearintrp and matmult on every cycle after the testbench's lead-in phase (its first 8 cycles), on its own copy. The testbench (tb_equiv_raygentop.v) and the block's behavioural model (dsp58_int8_dot.v) are in sim_reference/; you may read them, but editing them has no effect on the result.

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Inner-loop check: the front end of the real flow, then a cell census. NOT place-and-route.
# usage: bash check.sh <file.v>
# Reads, in the flow's order: blocks.vlib (every hard block of the architecture, declared from its
# models), primitives.vlib (the flow's library of memories, adders and multipliers), then the design;
# then elaborates with the flow's hierarchy check. A module that is not defined, a port name the
# block does not have, or a misspelled block name fails here as it would in the flow. Do not
# declare the target block in the design: blocks.vlib declares it, and a design that defines a module
# of the target block's name fails this check.
# Prints one pass/fail line, then the cell census of the whole design (the last "Number of cells"
# table yosys prints): soft arithmetic as $mul/$add/$sub, registers as $dff*/$sdff*, memories as
# $mem*, muxes as $mux/$pmux, and every instantiated module by name, which is where a hard-block
# instantiation shows up with its instance count.
set -u
f="${1:?usage: check.sh <file.v>}"
here="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
lib=""
[ -f "$here/blocks.vlib" ] && lib="read_verilog -lib $here/blocks.vlib; "
[ -f "$here/primitives.vlib" ] && lib="${lib}read_verilog -nomem2reg $here/primitives.vlib; "
target="$(sed -n 's|^// target block: *\([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_$]*\).*|\1|p' "$here/blocks.vlib" 2>/dev/null | head -1)"
if [ -n "$target" ] && grep -Eq "^[[:space:]]*module[[:space:]]+$target([^A-Za-z0-9_$]|$)" "$f"; then
  echo "FAIL: $f defines module $target; blocks.vlib declares it. Remove the module definition and keep the instances."
  exit 1
fi
out="$(yosys -p "${lib}read_verilog -sv -nolatches $f; hierarchy -check -auto-top -purge_lib; proc; opt; stat" 2>&1)"
rc=$?
if [ $rc -ne 0 ]; then
  echo "FAIL: does not synthesize (yosys exit $rc)"
  echo "$out" | grep -iE 'error|warning' | tail -15
  echo "$out" | tail -10
  exit 1
fi
echo "OK: synthesizes cleanly"
echo "Cell census (yosys stat, whole design):"
echo "$out" | awk '/Number of cells:/{buf=$0 "\n"; on=1; next}
                   on && /^[[:space:]]+[$A-Za-z_][^[:space:]]*[[:space:]]+[0-9]+[[:space:]]*$/ {buf=buf $0 "\n"; next}
                   on {on=0}
                   END{printf "%s", buf}' \
  | grep -E 'Number of cells|^[[:space:]]+(\$(mul|add|sub|mem|memrd|memwr|dff|dffe|sdff|sdffe|adff|adffe|mux|pmux)|[A-Za-z_])' || true
====
// dsp58_int8_dot: behavioural model of the arch mode dsp58_int8_dot_mode, which mimics the AMD DSP58
// with DSP_MODE = INT8, the INT8 vector dot product (AM004 v1.2.1 chapter 4), attributes AREG = BREG = 1,
// ACASCREG = BCASCREG = 1, MREG = 0, PREG = 1, OPMODEREG = 0, INMODEREG = 0, USE_MULT = MULTIPLY,
// AMULTSEL = A, BMULTSEL = B, ALUMODE = 0000, INMODE = 00000, CARRYINSEL = 000, CARRYIN = 0, and the
// C port tied to 0 inside the mode. Reference for every per-cycle question: AMD's simulation model
// /tools/Xilinx/Vivado/2024.1/data/verilog/src/unisims/DSP58.v (Vivado 2024.1; registers start at 0 in the
// initial block at lines 1241-1271), read in place and never copied.
// Element i (i = 0, 1, 2): a_i = A[9i+8:9i], 9 bits signed; b_i = B[8i+7:8i], 8 bits signed.
// dot = +/-a0*b0 +/- a1*b1 +/- a2*b2, where NEGATE[i] = 1 negates product i.
// Per clock edge: A2 <= A if CEA2 (cleared by RSTA); B2 <= B if CEB2 (cleared by RSTB);
// P <= f(OPMODE, dot of A2, B2 under NEGATE, P) if CEP (cleared by RSTP). A, B to P: 2 cycles; OPMODE
// and NEGATE to P: 1 cycle (they are not registered). P wraps at 58 bits. OPMODE is restricted to the
// four values below; on any other value at an edge that loads P, P becomes X.
// Signed two's complement throughout, as the real block; unsigned data must have a zero top bit on
// each operand. Every register starts at 0: AMD's simulation model starts its registers at 0 in the
// `initial` block(s) cited below (Vivado 2024.1). Resets are synchronous, active high, clear only the
// register(s) they name and win over that register's clock enable

Task

You are the acceptance reviewer. You did not plan or write this edit. Check the edit against the approved plan, the block's contract and the correctness rules.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: dsp58_int8_dot (precision INT9)
  • what it is: this mode copies the AMD DSP58 block in one configuration, "DSP_MODE = INT8, INT8 vector dot product" (AM004 v1.2.1 chapter 4 (p71-75), p9). It is not the whole DSP58: only the ports below exist, and the rest of the real block is fixed inside the mode (C = 0, ALUMODE = 0000, INMODE = 00000, CARRYINSEL = 000, CARRYIN = 0, D = 0, A[33:27] = 0, ASYNC_RST = 0, every cascade input 0, every clock enable and reset not listed as a port 0). Differences from the real block: the C port is tied to 0 inside the mode (the tile has 116 routable data inputs); OPMODE is restricted to the listed values; PCOUT, ACOUT, BCOUT, the carry, pattern-detect and XOR outputs are not exposed; timing figures and tile area are this project's, not AMD's.
  • computes: P = a0*b0 + a1*b1 + a2*b2; the fields are a0 = A[8:0], a1 = A[17:9], a2 = A[26:18], b0 = B[7:0], b1 = B[15:8], b2 = B[23:16] (each a is a 9-bit and each b an 8-bit two's complement number). That is one of the forms the block takes: OPMODE chooses the form at every clock edge (listed below).
  • input ports: CLK[1] (clock), A[27], B[24], OPMODE[9], NEGATE[3], RSTA[1], RSTB[1], RSTP[1], CEA2[1], CEB2[1], CEP[1]
  • output ports: P[58]
  • arithmetic: the block's operands and result are signed two's complement. A design operand narrower than its port must be widened to the port width: zero-extended if the design treats it as unsigned, sign-extended if signed. For unsigned data the top bit of each element's field must be zero (AM004 p103: "port A can hold up to three unsigned 8-bit values by setting the sign bits to 0 to indicate non-negative values."). So an unsigned operand may be at most 8 bits on each of a0, a1, a2 and 7 bits on each of b0, b1, b2; a wider unsigned operand has no exact representation on this block. P is a 58-bit value accumulated modulo 2^58. The result wraps at 58 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 58 bits (modulo 2^58), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on CLK, the block has exactly these registers. A2 loads A at a clock edge where CEA2 is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTA. B2 loads B at a clock edge where CEB2 is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTB. P loads the value OPMODE selects from the dot product of A2 and B2 (under NEGATE) and P at a clock edge where CEP is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTP; the output P is this register. The block has no path around a register.
  • timing: A and B reach P 2 clock edges after they are applied (applied before edge k, in P after edge k+1); OPMODE and NEGATE have no register before the one that drives P: the value present at a clock edge is in P after that edge (1 cycle).

  • control OPMODE: a per-cycle input, not registered; it acts at the clock edge that loads P. Legal values, and what the clock edge then does:

    • 000000101 (dot product): P <= dot
    • 000100101 (accumulate): P <= P + dot
    • 010000000 (hold): P <= P
    • 000000000 (zero): P <= 0
    • any other value: a clock edge that loads P under it leaves P unknown (X); the mode does not define it.
  • control NEGATE: a per-cycle input, not registered; it acts at the clock edge that loads P, on the products of the A2 and B2 contents at that edge. NEGATE[i] = 1 negates product i: dot = (NEGATE[0] ? -a0*b0 : a0*b0) + (NEGATE[1] ? -a1*b1 : a1*b1) + (NEGATE[2] ? -a2*b2 : a2*b2), with a and b the fields of A2 and B2. Every 3-bit value is legal.
  • resets and clock enables (resets RSTA, RSTB and RSTP; clock enables CEA2, CEB2 and CEP): each reset is synchronous and active high, clears only its own register to 0 at the clock edge that samples it high, and wins over that register's clock enable; each clock enable is active high; its register holds its value at a clock edge that samples the enable low. The block has no other reset and no other enable.
  • power-up: every register of the block starts at 0 (as the vendor's simulation model starts them), so P is defined from the first clock edge with or without a reset.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. A reset the rewrite does not need is tied to 0; a clock enable it does not need is tied to 1; OPMODE may be a constant legal value.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block at A, B, RSTA, RSTB, RSTP, CEA2, CEB2 and CEP: a path ends at a register of the block (setup)0.019
into the block at OPMODE and NEGATE, which have no input register: the path runs on through the block's delay to the register behind P (delay + setup)2.140 + 0.019 = 2.159
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of P (clock-to-Q)0.060
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path into OPMODE and NEGATE is not cut at the block's pins: it runs through the block to the register behind P, so the logic that drives them and the block's delay are in one stage.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design

The file raygentop.v is the edited design. The edit is the diff below, taken against the original source.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's resets, clock enables or per-cycle controls, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's resets (RSTA, RSTB and RSTP), clock enables (CEA2, CEB2 and CEP) and per-cycle controls (OPMODE and NEGATE) so that "same behavior" holds after every reset of the design, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds in soft logic start unknown; do not rely on their initial value. The block's own registers start at 0, as its power-up line states.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Check
  • The edit does what the approved plan says; any change outside the regions it names is explained by the plan.
  • Every block input port is connected, and the block's outputs drive what the plan says they drive.
  • The correctness rules hold for the edited source.

The synthesis checks and the reference simulation have passed; the remaining checks run after your review.

Output

  • If REJECT: the rule, the lines, what is wrong.
  • Write nothing before the verdict line; read the source first. The verdict is applied automatically, and your reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The approved plan

I'll start by reading the design source.

REGIONS: bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2875$349, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2876$350, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2879$353, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2882$356
SHARED_REDUCTION: the three output sums of bilinearintrp: r = i1r+i2r+i3r (raygentop.v:2884), g = i1g+i2g+i3g (2885), b = i1b+i2b+i3b (2886) — one block per sum
BLOCKS: 3
MAPPING:

  • Block dsp_r (red channel): CLK=clk; A = {1'b0, wnext, 1'b0, v, 1'b0, u} so a0=u(8b unsigned), a1=v(8b unsigned), a2=wnext(8b unsigned); B = {1'b0, rw, 1'b0, rv, 1'b0, ru} so b0=ru, b1=rv, b2=rw (7b unsigned each); OPMODE=9'b000000101; NEGATE=3'b000; RSTA=RSTB=RSTP=1'b0; CEA2=CEB2=CEP=1'b1; P → assign r = P[6:0].
  • Block dsp_g: same A, OPMODE, NEGATE, resets, enables; B = {1'b0, gw, 1'b0, gv, 1'b0, gu}; P → assign g = P[6:0].
  • Block dsp_b: same A, OPMODE, NEGATE, resets, enables; B = {1'b0, bw, 1'b0, bv, 1'b0, bu}; P → assign b = P[6:0].
  • wnext is new soft combinational logic: wire [7:0] wnext = 8'b11111111 - u - v; (the right-hand side of raygentop.v:2862, unregistered), with u, v the existing combinational mux outputs of the always @(selectuv or ...) block at raygentop.v:2818-2858, which stays exactly as it is.

PLAN:

What is converted and why it is exact

All nine multiplies in bilinearintrp (raygentop.v:2874-2882) are converted onto three dsp58_int8_dot blocks, one per colour channel. Each channel is already literally a 3-term dot product of three 8-bit unsigned values (ul, vl, wl) with three 7-bit unsigned values (rul/rvl/rwl, gul/…, bul/…), which is exactly the block's form: an unsigned operand may be 8 bits on each a field and 7 bits on each b field, so every operand fits with the required zero top bit and no decomposition or correction logic is needed.

Value equality: each product is at most 255*127 = 32385, exact in the 15-bit i* registers and exact in the block; the dot is at most 97155, which fits the model's 19-bit signed dot and the 58-bit P with no wrap. The outputs are 7-bit wires, so the original computes (i1r+i2r+i3r) in 15-bit arithmetic and keeps the low 7 bits; taking P[6:0] is the same value because reduction mod 2^15 then mod 2^7 equals reduction mod 2^7.

Visibility: ul, vl, wl, rul…bwl and i1r…i3b are declared at raygentop.v:2794-2816 and are read nowhere except inside the nine multiplies and the three output sums (raygentop.v:2874-2886); they are not ports and leave the module nowhere else. u and v stay as soft combinational signals (they also feed wnext), so nothing readable disappears into a block.

Edits for the editor (module bilinearintrp only, raygentop.v:2762-2887; the port list and port declarations are untouched):

  1. Delete the register declarations ul, vl, wl, i1b, i2b, i3b, i1g, i2g, i3g, i1r, i2r, i3r, rul, rvl, rwl, gul, gvl, gwl, bul, bvl, bwl (raygentop.v:2796-2816). Keep reg [7:0] u; reg [7:0] v;.
  2. Keep the combinational always @(selectuv or …) block (2818-2858) unchanged.
  3. Delete the whole always @(posedge clk) block (2860-2883) — both the latch-in assignments and the nine multiplies — and the three assign statements at 2884-2886.
  4. Add wire [7:0] wnext = 8'b11111111 - u - v; and wire [26:0] dot_a = {1'b0, wnext, 1'b0, v, 1'b0, u};.
  5. Instantiate the three blocks as in MAPPING, with wire [57:0] pr, pg, pb;, then assign r = pr[6:0]; assign g = pg[6:0]; assign b = pb[6:0];.
  6. Every input port of every block is connected by name as listed: unused resets tied to 1'b0, unused enables tied to 1'b1, OPMODE the constant legal value 9'b000000101 (dot product), NEGATE 3'b000 (all three products added).

Registers, reset and start-up

bilinearintrp has no reset port and no clock enable: at every clock edge the original loads ul/vl/wl/rul…bwl from u/v/255-u-v/ru… and loads i1r…i3b from the products of the previous edge's values. The block's A2/B2 replace the first rank (hence CEA2=CEB2=1, RSTA=RSTB=0) and P replaces the sum of the second rank (CEP=1, RSTP=0). wl is replaced by wnext registered inside A2, which is the same register stage as the original wl. All three blocks register their own copy of the same dot_a, matching the single shared ul/vl/wl rank. The design's registers start at 0 and the block's A2, B2, P start at 0, so r, g, b are 0 on the same cycles at start-up; since no reset of the design exists here, there is nothing further to reproduce, and the match holds on every cycle including the testbench's lead-in and drain phases.

Register stages, operands to result

  • Before: u/v/ru… → stage 1 (ul/vl/wl/rul… at raygentop.v:2862-2873) → stage 2 (i1r…i3b at 2874-2882) → combinational 3-input 15-bit add → r/g/b. Two register stages plus an adder on the output path.
  • After: u/v/ru… → stage 1 (A2/B2) → stage 2 (P, which holds the dot product itself) → bit slice P[6:0] → r/g/b. Two register stages, same latency on every cycle; the output adder is gone (the sum happens inside the block), so the path leaving the module is just P clock-to-Q.

Candidates left, with reasons

The nine matmult multiplies (raygentop.v:335-343, ids matmult.$mul$raygentop.v:335$15 through …343$23) are left, all for one reason: both operands are 16-bit unsigned, and this block represents an unsigned operand exactly only up to 8 bits on a0/a1/a2 and 7 bits on b0/b1/b2, so no 16x16 product fits on a block. An exact rewrite would need each operand split (2 chunks of 8 bits on A, 3 chunks of at most 7 bits on B) = 6 partial products per multiply, 54 in total = 18 blocks plus soft shift-and-add trees, against the 3 DSP tiles these multiplies occupy now. Furthermore the design truncates each product on its own before summing — Cx <= am11[30:15] + am12[30:15] + am13[30:15] (raygentop.v:348-350), so the low 15 bits are discarded per product — which means the block's accumulate mode cannot stand in for the channel sum anyway and each product would have to come out of the blocks separately. Not chosen: it is a large area loss with no path to a gain.

DESIGN_CHANGE: in matmult, the three channel sums discard the low 15 bits of each product before adding (raygentop.v:348-350). If the design instead summed the full 32-bit products and truncated once (Cx <= (am11+am12+am13)[30:15]), each channel would be a single dot product and the per-product partial sums could be accumulated inside one block per channel; the 16-bit operand widths would still need splitting, so the bigger win would come from narrowing Ax/Ay/Az to 8 bits unsigned and m11…m33 to 7 bits unsigned, after which each of Cx, Cy, Cz is exactly one dsp58_int8_dot, taking matmult from 3 DSP tiles to 3 with the adders removed, or from 9 multiplies to 3 dot products. Both are changes to what the design computes and are not part of this rewrite.
