The plan, in full 5405 chars · not approved

I'll read the source first.

REGIONS: NONE
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 0
MAPPING: none — this pass proposes no new rewrite; the kept candidate (revision 1, three dsp58_int8_dot blocks in bilinearintrp) stands as the run's result.
PLAN:

Why no further rewrite: nothing left to try that can win

I read raygentop.v again (the bilinearintrp region at 2762-2887 and the matmult region at 300-352) before deciding.

Candidate regions, converted or left

  • bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348 … …2882$356 (all nine): already converted by the kept candidate, onto three blocks, one per output sum (r, g, b). Nothing in that region is left unconverted, and the form in revision 1 is the minimal one for this block: each channel is three products, a block holds exactly three products, so three blocks is the floor; the two 8-bit B-side differences per channel are the fewest soft operations that fold w = 255-u-v out of the timed A path (the alternative factorings need either five products in one block, which does not fit, or the subtracts back on the A side, which is what the first pass measured at 4.75 ns). A further micro-variant exists — taking each difference mod 128 as a 7-bit subtract, legal because only P[6:0] leaves the module and a change of b by a multiple of 128 moves P by a multiple of 128 — but it saves on the order of one CLB, far inside the ~6.3% seed noise, so it is not worth a measurement.
  • matmult.$mul$raygentop.v:335$15 … …343$23 (all nine): left, for a correctness-plus-cost reason, the same one as before, now checked against the measured path. Both operands are 16-bit unsigned; this block represents an unsigned value exactly only in 8 bits on a0/a1/a2 and 7 bits on b0/b1/b2, so a 16×16 product must be split. The block sums its three products with no shift between them, so only partial products of equal weight may share a block. With A split 8+8 (weights 0, 8) and B split 7+7+2 (weights 0, 7, 14) the six partial-product weights are 0, 7, 8, 14, 15, 22 — all distinct, one block per partial product, 54 blocks. The best weight-colliding split (7+7+2 on both sides) gives nine partial products in five weight groups, 45 blocks. Either way the 3 DSP tiles these multiplies occupy today become 18 to 45 tiles (253779 each, against a whole-design area of 4.65e+06), plus full-width soft shift-and-add trees, because the design truncates each product individually (Cx <= am11[30:15] + am12[30:15] + am13[30:15], raygentop.v:348-350) and so the block's accumulate mode cannot absorb the channel sum either. That is a multiple-fold area loss with no path to a gain.

Why the delay cannot be pushed further through this block

The kept candidate's worst path at seed 1 is no longer in the converted region: it is fbdata~6 (flop, clock-to-Q 0.060) → 1.209 ns of routing → $mul~27.a[6] → 2.140 ns through a matmult DSP multiply → 0.828 ns of routing → $dff~36^Q~29.D (bit 29 of an am register), 4.281 ns arrival, 4.25699 ns period. Of that, 2.140 ns is the hard multiply itself and 2.04 ns is routing; the only part of it I could touch is the matmult multiply, and rewriting it onto dsp58_int8_dot is ruled out above. Any further improvement to bilinearintrp — whose A-side path now arrives at roughly 3.4 ns, about 0.87 ns of slack under the limiting path — cannot lower the period at all, and its remaining soft area (the u/v mux, which must stay outside because u and v are read by all three blocks, plus six 8-bit subtracts on untimed input-pad paths) is already near its floor. Area is likewise at its floor on the DSP side: 3 tiles for nine bilinearintrp products either way, since the native mode packs at most four 9×9 multiplies per tile and nine products need three tiles regardless.

So there is no remaining rewrite onto this block that is both correct and plausibly better than what is kept. The run should end and keep revision 1, whose area × delay is 1.97787e+07 against the baseline's 2.36893e+07 (−16.51%; A/A0 0.971, D/D0 0.860) — a margin well outside the ~6.3% seed-to-seed spread.

Register stages of the kept conversion, for the record

  • Before: u/v/ru…bw → stage 1 (ul, vl, wl, rul…bwl, raygentop.v:2862-2873) → stage 2 (i1r…i3b, 2874-2882) → combinational three-input 15-bit add → r/g/b. Two register stages with an adder tree after the last.
  • After: u/v → A2; ru…bw → one 8-bit subtract → B2 (stage 1) → P (stage 2, holding the dot product) → bit slice P[6:0] → r/g/b. Two register stages, identical latency every cycle, output adder gone.

DESIGN_CHANGE: in matmult, the three channel sums discard the low 15 bits of each product before adding (raygentop.v:348-350). If the design summed the full 32-bit products and truncated once (Cx <= (am11+am12+am13)[30:15]), each channel would be a single dot product whose partial products could be accumulated in one block per channel; the 16-bit operand widths would still force splitting, so the larger win would come from narrowing Ax/Ay/Az to 8 bits unsigned and m11…m33 to 7 bits unsigned, after which each of Cx, Cy, Cz is exactly one dsp58_int8_dot and the three adder trees disappear — which would also remove the multiply that now sets the period. Both change what the design computes and are outside this rewrite.
